\widetilde{\Omega}^{\prime}(\mathrm{Irr}({{{{\widetilde{\mathbf{G}}}}^{F}}}\mid\mathrm{Irr}_{\ell^{\prime}}({{{{\mathbf{G}}}^{F}}})))=\mathrm{Irr}({\widetilde{N}}\mid\mathrm{Irr}_{\ell^{\prime}}(N))
(see the discussion on degrees in
[
CS17a
, pp 180-181]
).
This finishes the proof of (a).

For the proof of (b), we can apply
Proposition

2.12
thanks to Conditions
\mathbf{A}(d)
and
\mathbf{B}(d)
. Hence, there exists a bijection

\Omega^{\prime}:\mathcal{G}_{d}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Irr}(N),

such that

(G\rtimes\Gamma_{\chi},G,\chi)\geq_{c}(N\rtimes\Gamma_{\chi},N,\Omega^{\prime}(\chi))\text{ for every }\chi\in\mathcal{G}_{d}.

Assuming
d=d_{\ell}(q)
for some odd prime
\ell
, as
\widetilde{\Omega}^{\prime}(\mathrm{Irr}({\widetilde{G}}\mid\mathrm{Irr}_{\ell^{\prime}}(G)))=\mathrm{Irr}(\widetilde{N}\mid\mathrm{Irr}_{\ell^{\prime}}(N))
the map
\Omega^{\prime}
satisfies
\Omega^{\prime}(\mathrm{Irr}_{\ell^{\prime}}(G))=\mathrm{Irr}_{\ell^{\prime}}(N)
by Clifford theory. ∎

5
The group
M
. Characters and Clifford theory

From now on we work with the group
{{\mathbf{G}}}\cong\mathrm{D}_{l,\mathrm{sc}}({\mathbb{F}})
(
l\geq 4
), seen as a subgroup of
\overline{{{\mathbf{G}}}}\cong\mathrm{B}_{l,\mathrm{sc}}({\mathbb{F}})
with common maximal torus
{\mathbf{T}}
, associated root system
\overline{\Phi}=\Phi(\overline{{{\mathbf{G}}}},{\mathbf{T}})
and root subgroups
{\mathbf{X}}_{\alpha}
(
{\alpha}\in\overline{\Phi}
), see
2.E
. Recall also the Frobenius endomorphism
F\colon{{\mathbf{G}}}\to{{\mathbf{G}}}
such that
{{{{\mathbf{G}}}^{F}}}=\mathrm{D}_{l,\mathrm{sc}}^{\epsilon}(q)
.

In this chapter we introduce first a finite subgroup
M\leq{{\mathbf{G}}}
depending on some integers
l_{1}
,
l_{2}=l-l_{1}
,
{\epsilon}_{1}=\pm 1
and
{\epsilon}_{2}={\epsilon}_{1}{\epsilon}
. The group
M
has a normal subgroup
M_{0}
of index
2\gcd(2,q-1)
, which is a central product
G_{1}.G_{2}
with
G_{i}\cong\mathrm{D}_{l_{i},{\mathrm{sc}}}^{{\epsilon}_{i}}(q)
in the sense of
Definition

3.17
. In an analysis split into two main cases, we also introduce in
Section

5.B
a group
{\underline{E}}(M)
acting on
M
that will allow us to make statements similar to the condition
A
(\infty)
for this group
M
. This will involve changing
F
into a slightly different
{{\nu F_{q}}}
that proves more suitable when dealing with
d
-tori.
Our main aim is to deduce from the knowledge of
\mathrm{Irr}(G_{i})
, the properties of
\mathrm{Irr}(M)
, mainly the statement in
Theorem

5.20
, an analogue of
\mathbf{A}(d)
for
M
. We show later in
Lemma

6.10
that the integers
l_{1}
,
{\epsilon}_{1}
can be chosen so that the associated group
M
contains
{\mathrm{N}}_{{{\mathbf{G}}}^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}}({\mathbf{S}})
for a Sylow
d
-torus
{\mathbf{S}}
of
({{\mathbf{G}}},{{\nu F_{q}}})
.

The present chapter is structured as follows. First, we define the group
M
and investigate the structure of
M
as a group, in particular, we introduce the normal subgroup
M_{0}\unlhd M
. Afterwards, we introduce a group
{\underline{E}}(M)
acting on
M
in
Section

5.B
.

Section

5.C
starts with collecting some basic observations on the characters of
M
and an approach how to study them via
M_{0}
, thus establishing some preliminary simplifications for the proof of
Theorem

5.20
. Since
M_{0}
is the central product
G_{1}.G_{2}
of two groups of type
\mathrm{D}
, we transfer in Section
5.D
the results on the character sets
\overline{\mathbb{T}}
,
{\mathbb{E}}
,
\mathbb{D}
from Chapter
3
to the characters of the direct factors of
M_{0}
. This leads to a partitioning of
\mathrm{Irr}(M)
into subsets along the characters of
M_{0}
and splitting the proof of
Theorem

5.20
according to those sets in Sections
5.E
and
5.F
.

5.A
The group
M

In the following we introduce a subgroup
{\mathbf{M}}
of
{{\mathbf{G}}}=\mathrm{D}_{l,\mathrm{sc}}({\mathbb{F}})
and then set
M:={\mathbf{M}}^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}
, defined in a general way from the integers
{\epsilon}_{1}
,
{\epsilon}_{2}
,
l_{1}
and
l_{2}
as well as the prime power
q
determining some Frobenius endomorphism
{{\nu F_{q}}}
. The conventions followed to define
{{\mathbf{G}}}_{1}
and
{{\mathbf{G}}}_{2}
in
Notation

5.1
are meant to limit the number of cases to review in proofs. The rôles of
{{\mathbf{G}}}_{1}
and
{{\mathbf{G}}}_{2}
are symmetric in the sense that a Sylow
d
-torus of
{{\mathbf{G}}}
will eventually be assumed to be included in one of them but both cases,
{{\mathbf{G}}}_{1}
or
{{\mathbf{G}}}_{2}
are permitted, see
Section

6.C
. The definitions made below may look arbitrary, but a glance at
Lemma

5.8
and its proof can already provide an explanation.

For a given Frobenius endomorphism
F^{\prime}
of
{{\mathbf{G}}}
recall the Lang map

{{\mathcal{L}_{F^{\prime}}}}:{{\mathbf{G}}}\longrightarrow{{\mathbf{G}}}\text{ given by }x\mapsto x^{-1}F^{\prime}(x).

Notation
\@upn
5.1

Let
{\epsilon}_{1},{\epsilon}_{2}\in\{\pm 1\}
and
l_{1},l_{2}\geq 1
with
{\epsilon}={\epsilon}_{1}{\epsilon}_{2}
and
l_{1}+l_{2}=l\geq 5
. Assume that

(i)

{\epsilon}_{1}=-1
if
{\epsilon}=-1
; and

(ii)

2\mid l_{1}
if
2\nmid l
and
({\epsilon}_{1},{\epsilon}_{2})=(-1,-1)
.

For
{{J_{1}:=\underline{l_{1}}}}
,
{{J_{2}:=\underline{l}\setminus J_{1}}}
and
i\in\{1,2\}
, let
{{{\overline{R}}_{i}}}:=\overline{\Phi}\cap\left\langle e_{j}\mid j\in J_{i}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{Z}}
,
{{R_{i}}}:=\overline{R}_{i}\cap\Phi
,
{{{\mathbf{T}}_{i}}}:={\mathbf{T}}\cap\left\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{\alpha}\mid{\alpha}\in\overline{R}_{i}\right\rangle
, and

{{{{\mathbf{G}}}_{i}}}:=\begin{cases}{\mathbf{T}}_{i}&\text{if }l_{i}=1,\\
\left\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{\alpha}\mid{\alpha}\in R_{i}\right\rangle&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Following
Notation

3.15
, we clearly have
{{\mathbf{G}}}_{1}={{\mathbf{G}}}_{\underline{l_{1}}}
and
{{\mathbf{G}}}_{2}\cong{{\mathbf{G}}}_{\underline{l_{2}}}
. Recall that for
k\geq 2
,
{{\mathbf{G}}}_{{\underline{k}}}\cong\mathrm{D}_{k,{\mathrm{sc}}}({\mathbb{F}})
, using the notation of
Definition

3.17
when
k\leq 3
.

Recall
{n_{1}^{\circ}=\mathbf{n}_{e_{1}}(\varpi)}
and set
{n_{2}^{\circ}:=\mathbf{n}_{e_{l}}(\varpi)}
. The above and the commutators given in
2.13
and
2.24
(d) yield at once the following.

Lemma
\@upn
5.2

(a)

[{{\mathbf{G}}}_{1},{{\mathbf{G}}}_{2}]=1
,
{{\mathbf{G}}}_{1}\cap{{\mathbf{G}}}_{2}=\left\langle h_{0}\right\rangle
with
{\mathbf{T}}\leq{{\mathbf{G}}}_{1}.{{\mathbf{G}}}_{2}
.

(b)

[n_{1}^{\circ},n_{2}^{\circ}]=h_{0}
,
n_{i}^{\circ}
normalizes
{{\mathbf{G}}}_{i}
and centralizes
{{\mathbf{G}}}_{3-i}
for
i=1,2
.

If
{{\gamma_{i}}}
(
i=1,2
) denotes the automorphism of
{\overline{\mathbf{G}}}
given by conjugation with
n^{\circ}_{i}
, then
{{\mathbf{G}}}_{i}
is
\gamma_{i}
-stable and
\gamma_{i}
induces a graph automorphism on
{{\mathbf{G}}}_{i}
. Moreover,
\gamma_{i}
defines an automorphism of
{{\mathbf{G}}}
. We also denote by
\gamma_{1}
the graph automorphism of
{\widetilde{\mathbf{G}}}
. Note that
\gamma_{2}
is the concatenation of
\gamma
and an inner automorphism of
{{\mathbf{G}}}
. As such
\gamma_{2}
acts also on
{\widetilde{\mathbf{G}}}
and by abuse of notation we denote this automorphism of
{\widetilde{\mathbf{G}}}
also by
\gamma_{2}
.

Before defining the group
{\mathbf{M}}\geq{\mathbf{M}}^{\circ}:={{\mathbf{G}}}_{1}.{{\mathbf{G}}}_{2}
and a slight replacement for the Frobenius endomorphism
F
we need to introduce the elements
\widehat{t}_{1},\widehat{t}_{2}\in{\mathbf{T}}
.

Lemma
\@upn
5.3

Set
{{v^{\circ}}}:=(n_{1}^{\circ})^{\frac{1-{\epsilon}_{1}}{2}}(n_{2}^{\circ})^{\frac{1-{\epsilon}_{2}}{2}}
,
{{{\mathbf{Z}}_{i}}}:=\left\langle\mathbf{h}_{J_{i}}(\varpi),h_{0}\right\rangle
and
{Z_{i}}:={\mathbf{Z}}_{i}^{v^{\circ}F_{q}}
. For
i=1,2
we choose some
{{\widehat{t}_{i}}}\in\mathcal{L}_{v^{\circ}F_{q}}^{-1}(h_{0})\cap{\mathbf{T}}\cap{{\mathbf{G}}}_{i}
with
\widehat{t}_{i}\in\operatorname{Z}({{\mathbf{G}}}_{i})
, when possible. Then:

(a)

if
i\in\{1,2\}
and
|Z_{i}|=2
, then
\widehat{t}_{i}\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{i}
;

(b)

if
({\epsilon}_{1},{\epsilon}_{2})=(-1,-1)
and
\{|Z_{1}|,|Z_{2}|\}=\{2,4\}
, then
\widehat{t}_{1}\in\operatorname{Z}({{\mathbf{G}}}_{1})
and
{F_{p}}(\widehat{t}_{1})=\widehat{t}_{1}
;

(c)

If
({\epsilon}_{1},{\epsilon}_{2})=(-1,-1)
and
\{|Z_{1}|,|Z_{2}|\}=\{2\}
, then one of the following holds

•

F_{p}(\widehat{t}_{1})=\widehat{t}_{1}
and
2\mid l_{1}
; or

•

F_{p}(\widehat{t}_{1}\widehat{t}_{2})=\widehat{t}_{1}\widehat{t}_{2}
and
2\nmid l_{1}l_{2}
.

We see that always
{\mathbf{Z}}_{i}\leq\operatorname{Z}({{\mathbf{G}}}_{i})
, and
{\mathbf{Z}}_{i}=\operatorname{Z}({{\mathbf{G}}}_{i})
unless
l_{i}=1
in which case
{{\mathbf{G}}}_{i}
is a torus.

Proof.

Observe that for every
I\subseteq{\underline{l}}
,
\mathbf{h}_{I}(\varpi)
is
F_{p}
-fixed if
4\mid(p-1)
or
2\mid|I|
.

In part (a),
\mathbf{h}_{J_{i}}(\varpi)\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{i}\setminus Z_{i}
and can therefore be chosen as
\widehat{t}_{i}
.

Then consider part (b). In this case
({\epsilon}_{1},{\epsilon}_{2})=(-1,-1)
and
\{|Z_{1}|,|Z_{2}|\}=\{2,4\}
. According to
2.24
,
\{|Z_{1}|,|Z_{2}|\}=\{2,4\}
implies that
l_{1}
and
l_{2}
have opposite parities. This leads to
2\nmid l_{1}+l_{2}=l
. The assumption in
5.1
(ii) implies
2\mid l_{1}
. According to
2.24
,
|Z_{1}|=2
and hence
\widehat{t}_{1}=\mathbf{h}_{J_{1}}(\varpi)
by (a). This proves part (b).

In part (c), we assume that
({\epsilon}_{1},{\epsilon}_{2})=(-1,-1)
and
|Z_{1}|=|Z_{2}|=2
. By part (a), we can choose
\widehat{t}_{i}=\mathbf{h}_{J_{i}}(\varpi)
for
i=1,2
. Such an element is
F_{p}
-fixed if and only if
l_{i}
is even, otherwise
F_{p}(\widehat{t}_{i})=h_{0}\widehat{t}_{i}
, see
3.4
.
If
2\nmid l
, then
2\mid l_{1}
by
5.1
(ii) and hence
F_{p}(\widehat{t}_{1})=\widehat{t}_{1}
. Otherwise
2\mid l=l_{1}+l_{2}
and
F_{p}(\widehat{t}_{1}\widehat{t}_{2})=\widehat{t}_{1}\widehat{t}_{2}
if
2\nmid l_{1}
.
∎

\@upn

|Z_{1}|
and

as the automorphism of

induced by conjugation by

, so that

for any

, and

extends to
{\widetilde{\mathbf{G}}}
as explained in
Section

for

. We then form
{{{{\nu F_{q}}}}}
the corresponding endomorphism of
{\widetilde{\mathbf{G}}}
. From
Lemma

2.23
and the choice of

in

one has clearly
{{\mathbf{G}}}^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}\cong{{\mathbf{G}}}^{F}\cong\mathrm{D}^{\epsilon}_{l,\mathrm{sc}}(q)
and

{{G_{i}:={{\mathbf{G}}}_{i}^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}}}\cong\mathrm{D}^{{\epsilon}_{i}}_{l_{i},{\mathrm{sc}}}(q)\text{ for }i=1,2

in the notation of
Definition

3.17
.
We also define

{{{\mathbf{M}}}}:=({{\mathbf{G}}}_{1}.{{\mathbf{G}}}_{2})\left\langle n_{1}^{\circ}n_{2}^{\circ}\right\rangle=({{\mathbf{G}}}_{1}.{{\mathbf{G}}}_{2})\left\langle n\right\rangle\geq{{M:={\mathbf{M}}^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}}}\geq{M^{\circ}:=({{\mathbf{G}}}_{1}.{{\mathbf{G}}}_{2})^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}}\geq{M_{0}:=G_{1}.G_{2}}.

Lemma
\@upn
5.5

Let
{v}
be defined as in
Table

5.1
. Then for
i=1,2
we have

(a)

{Z_{i}}={\mathbf{Z}}_{i}^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}=\left\langle\mathbf{h}_{J_{i}}(\varpi),h_{0}\right\rangle^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}
;

(b)

\mathcal{L}_{{{\nu F_{q}}}}(\widehat{t}_{1})=\mathcal{L}_{{{\nu F_{q}}}}(\widehat{t}_{2})=h_{0}
.

Proof.

We observe that
v^{-1}v^{\circ}\in{\mathbf{T}}
according to
Lemma

5.3
, so the actions of
v
and
v^{\circ}
on
{\mathbf{T}}
coincide. As a consequence we see that that the groups
Z_{i}
and the elements
\widehat{t}_{i}
defined using
v^{\circ}
satisfy similar properties with respect to
{{\nu F_{q}}}
.
∎

Lemma
\@upn
5.6
— Structure of
M
.

M^{\circ}=M_{0}\left\langle\widehat{t}_{1}\widehat{t}_{2}\right\rangle
and
M=M^{\circ}\left\langle n\right\rangle\lneq{{\mathbf{G}}}^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}
. Additionally,
G_{1}\unlhd M
and
G_{2}\unlhd M
.

Proof.

The equality
M^{\circ}=M_{0}\left\langle\widehat{t}_{1}\widehat{t}_{2}\right\rangle
comes from Lang’s theorem and
Lemma

5.2
(a). The inequality
{{{\mathbf{G}}}^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}}\neq M
holds since
{{{\mathbf{G}}}^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}}
is perfect. We also have
G_{i}\unlhd M^{\circ}\left\langle n\right\rangle
by the central product structure of
{\mathbf{M}}^{\circ}
and
Lemma

5.2
(b).

The equality
M=M^{\circ}\left\langle n\right\rangle
is true as soon as
[vF_{q},n]=1
.

Note
[n_{1}^{\circ}n_{2}^{\circ},F_{p}]=1
since
\mathbf{n}_{e_{j}}(\varpi)=\mathbf{h}_{e_{j}}(\varpi)\mathbf{n}_{e_{j}}(1)
for any
j\in{\underline{l}}
and therefore

\displaystyle[n_{i}^{\circ},F_{p}]:=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }4\mid(p-1),\\
h_{0}&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(5.1)

Recall
[n_{1}^{\circ},n_{2}^{\circ}]=h_{0}
, see
Lemma

5.2
(b).

If
v\in\{1,n\}
, then

[vF_{q},n]=[v,n][F_{q},n]=[F_{q},n]=1.

From
Table

5.1
we see that
v(v^{\circ})^{-1}\in\operatorname{Z}({{\mathbf{G}}}_{1}.{{\mathbf{G}}}_{2})^{F_{p}}
whenever
({\epsilon}_{1},{\epsilon}_{2})=(-1,-1)
or equivalently
v=n
. This verifies
[vF_{q},n]=1
whenever
{\epsilon}=1
.

According to
Table

5.1
it remains to consider the case where
{\epsilon}=-1
. Then
v=n_{1}^{\circ}
and we observe

\displaystyle[vF_{q},n]=[n_{1}^{\circ}F_{q},\widehat{t}_{j}n_{1}^{\circ}n_{2}^{\circ}]

\displaystyle=[n_{1}^{\circ}F_{q},\widehat{t}_{j}][n_{1}^{\circ}F_{q},n_{1}^{\circ}][n_{1}^{\circ}F_{q},n_{2}^{\circ}]=h_{0}[F_{q},n_{1}^{\circ}][n_{1}^{\circ},n_{2}^{\circ}][F_{q},n_{2}^{\circ}]=

\displaystyle=h_{0}[F_{q},n_{1}^{\circ}]h_{0}[F_{q},n_{2}^{\circ}]=1\text{ by (\ref{eq51}\immediate) above.}

This finishes our proof.
∎

Lemma
\@upn
5.7
— Action of
\widetilde{T}
on
M
.

Set
{{\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}}:=\mathcal{L}^{-1}_{{{\nu F_{q}}}}(\operatorname{Z}({{\mathbf{G}}}))\cap{\mathbf{T}}
,

{{\widetilde{T}}}:=({\mathbf{T}}\operatorname{Z}({\widetilde{\mathbf{G}}}))^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}
,

{T_{0}}:=G_{1}.G_{2}\cap{\mathbf{T}}
and let
{{\widetilde{t}_{i}}}\in\mathcal{L}_{{{\nu F_{q}}}}^{-1}(\mathbf{h}_{J_{i}}(\varpi))\cap{\mathbf{T}}_{i}
for
i=1,2
. Then

(a)

\widetilde{T}
and
\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}
induce the same automorphisms on
{{{\mathbf{G}}}^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}}
;

(b)

\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}=\left\langle\widetilde{t}_{1}\widetilde{t}_{2},\widehat{t}_{1},\widehat{t}_{2}\right\rangle T_{0}
;

(c)

\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}
and
\widetilde{T}
normalize
M
,
M^{\circ}
,
M_{0}
,
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
.

Proof.

Part (a) is standard, see
2.14
and
2.15
.

For part (b), we have
\mathbf{h}_{\underline{l}}(\varpi)=\mathbf{h}_{J_{1}}(\varpi)\mathbf{h}_{J_{2}}(\varpi)
and therefore
\widetilde{t}_{1}\widetilde{t}_{2}
satisfies
\mathcal{L}_{{{\nu F_{q}}}}(\widetilde{t}_{1}\widetilde{t}_{2})=\mathbf{h}_{{\underline{l}}}(\varpi)
. By
Lemma

5.6
, we have
{\mathbf{T}}^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}=T_{0}\left\langle\widehat{t}_{1}\widehat{t}_{2}\right\rangle
. On the other hand
\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}/{\mathbf{T}}^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}
is isomorphic to
\operatorname{Z}({{\mathbf{G}}})
by
\mathcal{L}_{{{\nu F_{q}}}}
, so we indeed get
\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}={\mathbf{T}}^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}\left\langle\widetilde{t}_{1}\widetilde{t}_{2},\widehat{t}_{1}\right\rangle
since by
Lemma

5.5
we are adding elements whose images under
\mathcal{L}_{{{\nu F_{q}}}}
generate
\operatorname{Z}({{\mathbf{G}}})
, see
2.24
(a).

We consider part (c). Recall
G_{1}\unlhd M
and
G_{2}\unlhd M
from
Lemma

5.6
.
Now
\widehat{t}_{1}
acts on
G_{1}
as a diagonal automorphism associated with
h_{0}[\operatorname{Z}({{\mathbf{G}}}_{1}),{{\nu F_{q}}}]
and
\widetilde{t}_{1}
acts on
G_{1}
as a diagonal automorphism associated with
\mathbf{h}_{J_{1}}(\varpi)[\operatorname{Z}({{\mathbf{G}}}_{1}),{{\nu F_{q}}}]
in the parametrization of
Notation

2.14
. On the other hand,
[\widehat{t}_{1},{{\mathbf{G}}}_{2}]=[\widetilde{t}_{1},{{\mathbf{G}}}_{2}]=1
. We can describe similarly the action of
\widetilde{t}_{2}
and
\widehat{t}_{2}
on
G_{2}
. We observe
[n,\widetilde{t}_{1}\widetilde{t}_{2}]\in\left\langle\widehat{t}_{1}\widehat{t}_{2}\right\rangle M_{0}=M^{\circ}
. Conjugation with
\widetilde{T}
and
\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}
then stabilises
M
,
{\mathbf{T}}
,
{{\mathbf{G}}}_{1}
and
{{\mathbf{G}}}_{2}
by
Lemma

5.6
and we get our claim.
∎

Before going further into describing
M
and some of its automorphisms we show below the relevance to our work around Sylow
d
-tori of
({{\mathbf{G}}},F)
for
d\geq 3
. In particular we show that for non-doubly regular
d
’s one can almost always build a group
M
such that one of the two groups
{{\mathbf{G}}}_{1}
and
{{\mathbf{G}}}_{2}
contains a Sylow
d
-torus of
({{\mathbf{G}}},{{\nu F_{q}}})
and
d
is doubly regular for that
({{\mathbf{G}}}_{j},{{\nu F_{q}}})
, see case (iii) of
Lemma

5.8
.

Recall that
{\epsilon}\in\{\pm 1\}
with
{{{{\mathbf{G}}}^{F}}}=\mathrm{D}^{\epsilon}_{l,\mathrm{sc}}(q)
. For
\zeta\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
a primitive
d
-th root of unity we denote by
{{a_{(\mathbf{G},F)}(d)}}
the multiplicity of
\zeta
as a root of the polynomial order

P_{({{\mathbf{G}}},F)}(X)=X^{l^{2}-l}(X^{2}-1)(X^{4}-1)\cdots(X^{2l-2}-1)(X^{l}-{\epsilon}),

see
[
GM
, Table 1.3]
.

Lemma
\@upn
5.8

We keep
{{{{\mathbf{G}}}^{F}}}=\mathrm{D}_{l,{\mathrm{sc}}}^{\epsilon}(q)
with
l\geq 5
and take
d\geq 3
. Then one of the following three possibilities occurs:

(i)

d
is doubly regular for
({{\mathbf{G}}},F)
;

(ii)

a_{({{\mathbf{G}}},F)}(d)\leq 1
and therefore
a_{({{\mathbf{G}}},F)}(dm)=0
for any odd
m\geq 3
; or

(iii)

there exist
j\in\{1,2\}
,
l_{1},l_{2}>0
and
{\epsilon}_{1},{\epsilon}_{2}\in\{\pm 1\}
with
l_{1}+l_{2}=l
,
{\epsilon}={\epsilon}_{1}{\epsilon}_{2}
determining
{{\mathbf{G}}}_{1}
,
{{\mathbf{G}}}_{2}
and
\nu
as in
Notation

5.1
and
Definition

5.4
such that
a_{({{\mathbf{G}}},F)}(d)=a_{({{\mathbf{G}}}_{j},{{\nu F_{q}}})}(d)
,
l_{j}\geq 4
and
d
is doubly regular for
({{\mathbf{G}}}_{j},{{\nu F_{q}}})
.

Proof.

In the following, whenever
k\geq 2
and
\delta=\pm 1
we write
a_{k,\delta}(d)
for the multiplicity of a given
d
-th root of unity as a root of

(X^{2}-1)(X^{4}-1)\cdots(X^{2k-2}-1)(X^{k}-\delta)=(X^{2}-1)\cdots(X^{2k}-1)/(X^{k}+\delta).

Then
a_{({{\mathbf{G}}},F)}(d)=a_{l,{\epsilon}}(d)
.

Let us check first that if
a_{l,{\epsilon}}(d)\leq 1
, then
a_{l,{\epsilon}}(dm)=0
for any odd
m\geq 3
. Let
\zeta\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
be a
dm
-th root of 1. If
a_{l,{\epsilon}}(dm)\geq 1
, then
\zeta
is a root of
X^{l}-{\epsilon}
or some
X^{2i}-1
for
1\leq i\leq l-1
. In the first case
\zeta^{m}
is then also a root of
X^{l}-{\epsilon}
and since
dm\mid 2l
,
d
divides the even integer
2i_{0}=2l/m
, so
\zeta^{m}
is a root of
X^{2i_{0}}-1
with
1\leq i_{0}\leq l-1
, leading to
a_{l,{\epsilon}}(d)\geq 2
. In the second case, we get
dm\mid 2i
, so
\zeta^{m}
is a root of both
X^{2i}-1
and
X^{2i^{\prime}}-1
for
i^{\prime}
the integer
i/m
, implying again
a_{l,{\epsilon}}(d)\geq 2
.

We now assume that (i) and (ii) are not satisfied, that is
a_{l,{\epsilon}}(d)\geq 2
and
d
is not doubly regular for
({{\mathbf{G}}},F)
, i.e.,
d\nmid 2l
or it does but
(-1)^{2l/d}\neq{\epsilon}
, see
Definition

4.4
.

Set
d_{0}=d
if
d
is odd,
d_{0}=d/2
if
d
is even. Set
k:=a_{l,{\epsilon}}(d)d_{0}
and set
\delta:=(-1)^{a_{l,{\epsilon}}(d)}
if
2\mid d
, and
\delta=1
otherwise. It is easy to see that
d
being not doubly regular implies

\displaystyle a_{l,{\epsilon}}(d)=a_{k,\delta}(d)=\Big\lfloor\frac{l-1}{d_{0}}\Big\rfloor\ \ \text{ and therefore }\ \ k=d_{0}\cdot\Big\lfloor\frac{l-1}{d_{0}}\Big\rfloor.

(5.2)

This can be checked directly on the polynomials recalled above or by arguing on the form of regular elements of order
d
in the Weyl groups of
{{\mathbf{G}}}
and
{{\mathbf{G}}}_{k}
, see
Lemma

4.5
.

Recall
l\geq 5
,
d\geq 3
and hence
d_{0}\geq 2
. So
4\leq d_{0}\cdot a_{l,{\epsilon}}(d)=k=d_{0}\cdot\lfloor\frac{l-1}{d_{0}}\rfloor\leq l-1
. Let us show that we can choose
({\epsilon}_{1},l_{1}),({\epsilon}_{2},l_{2})
such that the assumptions from
Notation

5.1
are satisfied and
(\delta,k)\in\{({\epsilon}_{1},l_{1}),({\epsilon}_{2},l_{2})\}
. Indeed if
\delta=1
the choice
({\epsilon}_{1},l_{1})=(\delta,k)
clearly satisfies
5.1
(i) and (ii). If
\delta=-1={\epsilon}
then one takes
({\epsilon}_{2},l_{2})=(\delta,k)
while
5.1
(ii) is empty. Finally, when
\delta=-1=-{\epsilon}
then
5.1
(i) is empty and one can always take
({\epsilon}_{1},l_{1})
or
({\epsilon}_{2},l_{2})=(\delta,k)
to satisfy the parity condition of
5.1
(ii).

We then get (iii) by taking
j\in\{1,2\}
such that
({\epsilon}_{j},l_{j})=(\delta,k)
since (
5.2
) above shows that the multiplicity of the
d
-th cyclotomic polynomial in the order of
({{\mathbf{G}}}_{j},{{\nu F_{q}}})
is the same as in the one of
({{\mathbf{G}}},F)
while
d
is clearly doubly regular for
({{\mathbf{G}}}_{j},{{\nu F_{q}}})
by choosing
2k=2d_{0}a_{l,{\epsilon}}(d)
and
\delta
.
∎

The above has given a hint on how
M
will be used in the next chapter to verify Conditions
\mathbf{A}(d)
and
\mathbf{B}(d)
. We give below a few more group-theoretic properties of
M
that won’t be used but explain that this construction is more natural, and less new, than it seems.

Remark 5.9
.

(a)

If
(l_{1},{\epsilon}_{1})\neq(l_{2},{\epsilon}_{2})
, then the group
M
is a maximal subgroup of
{{\mathbf{G}}}^{{\nu F_{q}}}
, a member of the family
\mathcal{C}_{1}
in Aschbacher’s classification, see
[
MT
, §18]
.

(b)

Recall
\pi_{\operatorname{SO}}\colon{{\mathbf{G}}}\to{\operatorname{SO}}_{2l}({\mathbb{F}})
the reduction mod
\left\langle h_{0}\right\rangle
. We have
\left\langle h_{0}\right\rangle\leq M
and
\pi_{\operatorname{SO}}({\mathbf{M}})={\mathrm{C}}_{{\operatorname{SO}}_{2l}({\mathbb{F}})}(s)
for
s:=\pi_{{\operatorname{SO}}}(\mathbf{h}_{J_{2}}(\varpi))
, a centraliser of an involution in
{\operatorname{SO}}_{2l}({\mathbb{F}})
.

(c)

Let
j\in\{1,2\}
such that
l_{j}\geq 2
and therefore there exists
{{\mathbf{L}}}
a
{{\nu F_{q}}}
-stable Levi subgroup of
{\mathbf{M}}^{\circ}
such that
[{{\mathbf{L}}},{{\mathbf{L}}}]={{\mathbf{G}}}_{j}
. Then
{\mathbf{M}}
satisfies

{\mathbf{M}}={\mathrm{N}}_{{{\mathbf{G}}}}([{{\mathbf{L}}},{{\mathbf{L}}}]).

Indeed, computations with the roots of
{{\mathbf{G}}}
allow us to see
{\mathbf{M}}^{\circ}=[{{\mathbf{L}}},{{\mathbf{L}}}]\,{\mathrm{C}}^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{G}}}([{{\mathbf{L}}},{{\mathbf{L}}}])
.
This group is normal in
{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathbf{G}}}([{{\mathbf{L}}},{{\mathbf{L}}}])
and we check easily
{\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathbf{G}}}({\mathbf{M}}^{\circ},{{\mathbf{G}}}_{3-j})={\mathbf{M}}^{\circ}\left\langle n\right\rangle
. This leads to
{\mathbf{M}}={\mathrm{N}}_{{{\mathbf{G}}}}([{{\mathbf{L}}},{{\mathbf{L}}}]){\text{ and }}

M={\mathrm{N}}_{{\mathbf{G}}}([{{\mathbf{L}}},{{\mathbf{L}}}])^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}.

If moreover for some odd prime
\ell
,
{{\mathbf{L}}}
is
d
-split and there exists a
d
-cuspidal character
\zeta\in\operatorname{Uch}({{\mathbf{L}}}^{{\nu F_{q}}})
for
d=d_{\ell}(q)
, then
({{\mathbf{L}}},\zeta)
forms a unipotent
d
-cuspidal pair of
({{\mathbf{G}}},{{\nu F_{q}}})
defining an
\ell
-block
B
of
{{\mathbf{G}}}^{{\nu F_{q}}}
, see
[
CE
, Thm 22.9]
. Certain subgroups of
M
are studied in
[
CE99
]
and are shown to control the
\ell
-fusion in
B
, see
[
CE99
, Prop. 5]
. When moreover
{{\mathbf{L}}}
is a minimal
d_{\ell}(q)
-split Levi subgroup as is the case in our applications of Ch. 6, then
M
contains a Sylow
\ell
-subgroup of
{{\mathbf{G}}}^{{\nu F_{q}}}
and controls the fusion of
\ell
-subgroups of
{{\mathbf{G}}}^{{\nu F_{q}}}
.

5.B
Some groups of automorphisms of
M

We now define the automorphism group
{\underline{E}}({\mathbf{M}})
as a slight variant of
{\underline{E}}({{\mathbf{G}}})
already encountered in the preceding sections. We then restrict it in a manner similar to the construction of
{\underline{E}}(G)
to obtain the finite groups
{\underline{E}}(M)
,
{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})
, and other variants suitable for
{{\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{439166}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{365185}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}}:=M\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}
.

Our further considerations are divided into two cases setting apart the case corresponding to the last line of Table
5.1
.
With the definition of
{\underline{E}}({{\mathbf{G}}})
as bijective group homorphisms we can form the abstract group
{{\mathbf{G}}}\rtimes{\underline{E}}({{\mathbf{G}}})
.

Definition
\@upn
5.10

Let
{\underline{E}}({\mathbf{M}})\leq{{\mathbf{G}}}\rtimes{\underline{E}}({{\mathbf{G}}})
be the subgroup given by

{{{\underline{E}}({\mathbf{M}})}}:=\begin{cases}\left\langle F_{p},\gamma\right\rangle={\underline{E}}({{\mathbf{G}}})&\text{if }n\in\operatorname{Z}({{\mathbf{G}}}_{1}.{{\mathbf{G}}}_{2})n_{1}^{\circ}n_{2}^{\circ},\\
\left\langle F_{p}^{2},\widehat{t}_{1}\gamma\right\rangle&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Let
{{{\underline{E}}(M)}}
, resp.
{{{\underline{E}}(\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{439166}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{365185}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits})}}
be the subgroup of
\mathrm{Aut}({\widetilde{\mathbf{G}}}^{{{\nu F_{q}}}})
, respectively of
\mathrm{Aut}({\widetilde{\mathbf{G}}}^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits})
, obtained by restriction of
{\underline{E}}({\mathbf{M}})
. Set

{{{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ}):={\underline{E}}(M)\left\langle n,h_{0}\right\rangle}}\leq{{{\mathbf{G}}}^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}}{\underline{E}}(M)\leq{{{\mathbf{G}}}^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}}\rtimes\mathrm{Aut}({{{\mathbf{G}}}^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}}),

so that
M{\underline{E}}(M)=M^{\circ}{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})
(see
Lemma

5.6
). Set
{\underline{E}}({\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{439166}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{365185}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}^{\circ}):={\underline{E}}(\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{439166}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{365185}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits})\left\langle n,h_{0}\right\rangle\leq{\widetilde{\mathbf{G}}}^{{\nu F_{q}}}\rtimes\mathrm{Aut}({\widetilde{\mathbf{G}}}^{{\nu F_{q}}})
.

From
Lemma

5.3
(a–b) it is easy to see that the first case considered above corresponds to the first five lines of Table

, while the second case corresponds to the last line where

.
We now gather information on those automorphism groups, starting with the first case of the above Definition.

Lemma
\@upn
5.13

Assume
Hypothesis

5.11
. Then:

(a)

{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})\cap M^{\circ}=\left\langle h_{0}\right\rangle
and
[{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ}),{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})]\leq\left\langle h_{0}\right\rangle
;

(b)

For
{Z}:=({\mathbf{Z}}_{1}.{\mathbf{Z}}_{2})^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}
the Sylow
2
-subgroup of
{\mathrm{C}}_{{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})}(Z)
is abelian.

Proof.

The inclusion
[{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ}),{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})]\leq\left\langle h_{0}\right\rangle
is clear from
Table

5.2
. We also have
(n_{1}^{\circ}n_{2}^{\circ})^{2}=h_{0}
by
2.24
(d). Then
n^{2}\in\left\langle h_{0}\right\rangle
by
2.24
(b) and since
n\in\operatorname{Z}({{\mathbf{G}}}_{1}.{{\mathbf{G}}}_{2})n_{1}^{\circ}n_{2}^{\circ}
. The overgroup
M^{\circ}
acts by 2 or 4 diagonal automorphisms on
G_{1}.G_{2}
while

{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})/\left\langle h_{0}\right\rangle=\left\langle\gamma,n\left\langle h_{0}\right\rangle,F_{p}\right\rangle
acts by the graph and field automorphisms
\gamma_{1}
,
\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}
and
F_{p}
on
G_{1}.G_{2}
. The classification of automorphisms of quasisimple groups makes that
{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})\cap M^{\circ}
acts trivially on
G_{1}.G_{2}
and equals
\left\langle h_{0}\right\rangle
since both
n^{2}
,
[F_{p},n]
and
[\gamma_{1},n]
belong to
\left\langle h_{0}\right\rangle
. This proves (a).

For part (b) set
C:={\mathrm{C}}_{{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})}(Z)=\left\langle h_{0},{\mathrm{C}}_{\left\langle\gamma,F_{p},n\right\rangle}(Z)\right\rangle
. If
|Z_{1}|=|Z_{2}|=4
, then

C\in\{\left\langle F_{p},h_{0}\right\rangle,\left\langle F_{p}\gamma_{1},h_{0}\right\rangle,\left\langle F_{p}n,h_{0}\right\rangle,\left\langle F_{p}n\gamma_{1},h_{0}\right\rangle\}

using again the description of the automorphisms induced on
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
.
In all cases,
C
is abelian.

Assume
{\epsilon}_{1}=1
and hence
{\epsilon}_{2}=1
. By the above we can assume
2\in\{|Z_{1}|,|Z_{2}|\}
and even
|Z_{1}|=2
. Then
2\nmid f
according to
2.24
(b) and hence
\left\langle\gamma,n,h_{0}\right\rangle
is the Sylow
2
-subgroup of
{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})
. If additionally
|Z_{2}|=4
, then
\left\langle\gamma,h_{0}\right\rangle
is the Sylow
2
-subgroup of
C
. If
|Z_{1}|=|Z_{2}|=2
and hence
|Z|=4
, then
C\leq\left\langle n,h_{0}\right\rangle
which is again abelian.

Next we consider the case where
{\epsilon}=1
and
{\epsilon}_{1}={\epsilon}_{2}=-1
. By assumption
2\in\{|Z_{1}|,|Z_{2}|\}
. According to
Lemma

5.3
,
|Z_{1}|=2
. The results in
Table

5.2
show that
{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})=\left\langle F_{p},\gamma_{1},n\right\rangle
is abelian, since the generators then commute with each other.

Next, assume
{\epsilon}=-1
. As above, we can assume that
2\in\{|Z_{1}|,|Z_{2}|\}
.
If
|Z_{1}|=2
, then
n=\widehat{t}_{1}n_{1}^{\circ}n_{2}^{\circ}
. According to
Table

5.2
,
[\gamma_{1},n]=1
in this case and the group
\left\langle\gamma_{1},n,h_{0}\right\rangle
is abelian. If additionally
|Z_{2}|=2
, then
2\nmid f
and
\left\langle\gamma_{1},n,h_{0}\right\rangle
is the Sylow
2
-subgroup of
{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})
.
If
|Z_{2}|=4
, then
C=\left\langle\gamma_{1},nF_{p}\right\rangle
or
C=\left\langle\gamma_{1},F_{p}\right\rangle
. Since in this case
[\gamma_{1},n]=1
, the group
\left\langle\gamma_{1},n,h_{0}\right\rangle
is abelian.
It remains to consider the case where
|Z_{1}|=4
and
|Z_{2}|=2
. In this case,
2\nmid f
and
n=\widehat{t}_{2}n_{1}^{\circ}n_{2}^{\circ}
.
Then the group
\left\langle\gamma_{1},n,h_{0}\right\rangle
is a Sylow
2
-subgroup of
{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})
and the centralizer of
Z_{1}.Z_{2}
in this group is
\left\langle\gamma_{1}n,h_{0}\right\rangle
, abelian again.
∎

Lemma
\@upn
5.14
— Comparison of
{\underline{E}}(M)
,
{\underline{E}}(G_{1})
and
{\underline{E}}(G_{2})
.

Assume
Hypothesis

5.11
. Let
{\underline{E}}(M)
be as in
Definition

5.10
.
For
i=1,2
set
{\underline{E}}(G_{i}):=\left\langle\gamma_{i},F_{p}\right\rangle\leq\mathrm{Aut}(G_{i})
, and let

{{{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})}}\leq\mathrm{Aut}(G_{i})
be the subgroup of automorphisms of
G_{i}
induced by
{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})
. Then

{\underline{E}}(G_{1})={\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{1}){\text{ and }}{\underline{E}}(G_{2})={\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{2}).

Proof.

Recall that
n
acts as
\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}
on
G_{1}.G_{2}
, see
Lemma

5.12
(a). Hence
{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{1})=\left\langle\gamma_{1},F_{p}\right\rangle
and
{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{2})=\left\langle\gamma_{2},F_{p}\right\rangle
. This leads to the statement.∎

In the following, we verify some adaptations of the above statement to the missing case corresponding to the last line of Table
5.1
.

Hypothesis
\@upn

Assume
{\epsilon}=-1
and
\{|Z_{1}|,|Z_{2}|\}=\{4\}
.

Recall
f
denotes the integer with
q=p^{f}
.

.

If

5.15
holds, then
2\nmid f
and
(p-1)_{2}=2
, see
2.24
(c).

Lemma
\@upn

Assume
Hypothesis

.

(a)

Then
n=\widehat{t}_{1}n_{1}^{\circ}n_{2}^{\circ}
acts on

as

and

some

{\underline{E}}(M)

(d)

Recall
\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{439166}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{365185}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}:=M\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}
and
{{{\underline{E}}(\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{439166}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{365185}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits})}}\leq\mathrm{Aut}({{{\mathbf{G}}}^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits})
the subgroup obtained by restricting
{\underline{E}}({\mathbf{M}})
to
{{{\mathbf{G}}}^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}
.
Then
{\underline{E}}(\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{439166}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{365185}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits})
stabilizes
\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}
,
M
,
G_{i}\left\langle\widehat{t}_{i},\widetilde{t}_{i}\right\rangle
and
\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{439166}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{365185}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}
.
Analogously,
{\underline{E}}(\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{439166}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{365185}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits})\left\langle n,h_{0}\right\rangle
stabilizes
\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}M_{0}
.

Proof.

We have
v=n_{1}^{\circ}
and
n=\widehat{t}_{1}n_{1}^{\circ}n_{2}^{\circ}
as defined by the last row of
Table

5.1
and the actions are clear from
Lemma

5.2
(b). This gives (a).

According to
Remark

5.16
,
(q-1)_{2}=(p-1)_{2}=2
and
(p+1)_{2}=(q+1)_{2}
. So for
\zeta
a
2(p+1)
-th root of
1
in
{\mathbb{F}^{\times}}
,
\mathbf{h}_{e_{1}}(\zeta)
satisfies
[\mathbf{h}_{e_{1}}(\zeta),vF]=[\mathbf{h}_{e_{1}}(\zeta),\gamma F]=\mathbf{h}_{e_{1}}(\zeta^{-q}\zeta^{-1})=h_{0}
. Hence we can choose
\widehat{t}_{1}=\mathbf{h}_{e_{1}}(\zeta)
. The order of
\zeta
divides
p^{2}-1
and hence
[\widehat{t}_{1},F_{p}^{2}]=1
. This shows part (b).

We see that
\gamma(\widehat{t}_{1})=\mathbf{h}_{e_{1}}(\zeta^{-1})=\widehat{t}_{1}^{-1}
hence
(\widehat{t}_{1}\gamma)^{2}=1
. Furthermore,
F_{p}^{2}
has odd order
f
as an automorphism of
{{\mathbf{G}}}^{{\nu F_{q}}}
, and commutes with
\widehat{t}_{1}\gamma_{1}
. We get that
{\underline{E}}(M)
is abelian, even cyclic of order
2f
since
\widehat{t}_{1}\gamma
has order
2
by the above. So we get the first claim of (c).

We have
[n,F_{p}^{2}]=1
since
[\widehat{t}_{1},F_{p}^{2}]=[n_{1}^{\circ}n_{2}^{\circ},F_{p}^{2}]=1
by
Lemma

5.3
, and we see

[n,\widehat{t}_{1}\gamma_{1}]=[\widehat{t}_{1}n_{1}^{\circ}n_{2}^{\circ},\widehat{t}_{1}\gamma_{1}]=[\widehat{t}_{1}n_{1}^{\circ},\widehat{t}_{1}\gamma_{1}][n_{2}^{\circ},\widehat{t}_{1}\gamma_{1}]=h_{0}.

This leads to
[n,{\underline{E}}(M)]=\left\langle h_{0}\right\rangle
, as claimed in (c). We see that
{\underline{E}}(M)
stabilises
\left\langle n\right\rangle
. For every
i\in\{1,2\}
, the groups
{{\mathbf{G}}}_{i}
and
G_{i}
are
\left\langle F_{p}^{2},\widehat{t}_{1}\gamma_{1}\right\rangle
-stable.
This completes the proof of part (c).

By definition
{\underline{E}}(M)
acts on
{\mathbf{T}}_{0}
and stabilises
\operatorname{Z}({{\mathbf{G}}})
. This implies that
{\underline{E}}(M)
stabilizes
{{{\mathbf{G}}}^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}
,
M
and
M\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}
. This shows part (d).
∎

The following gathers results easily obtained from Lemmas
5.17
and
2.24
.

\{|Z_{1}|,|Z_{2}|\}

v

n

Table 5.3:
Commutators of some elements in
M{\underline{E}}(M)

Lemma
\@upn
5.18

Assume
Hypothesis

5.15
.

(a)

[{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ}),{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})]=\left\langle h_{0}\right\rangle
and
{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})\cap M^{\circ}=\left\langle h_{0}\right\rangle
;

(b)

{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})/\left\langle h_{0}\right\rangle
is abelian and
{\mathrm{C}}_{{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})}(Z_{1}.Z_{2})
is abelian.

Proof.

Recall that by
Hypothesis

5.15
,
{\epsilon}=-1
and
\{|Z_{1}|,|Z_{2}|\}=\{4\}
, hence
2\nmid f
. The group
{\underline{E}}(M)=\left\langle F_{p}^{2},\widehat{t}_{1}\gamma\right\rangle
is abelian according to
Lemma

5.17
(c).
Table

5.3
implies
[{\underline{E}}(M),n]=\left\langle h_{0}\right\rangle
.

Recall
{\underline{E}}(M)\cap M=1
. Using
\gamma(\widehat{t}_{1})=\widehat{t}_{1}^{-1}
from the proof of Lemma
5.17(c)
and then
2.24
(d), we get
n^{2}=(\widehat{t}_{1}n_{1}^{\circ}n_{2}^{\circ})^{2}=(n_{1}^{\circ}n_{2}^{\circ})^{2}=h_{0}
. This gives

\left\langle n\right\rangle\cap M^{\circ}=\left\langle n^{2}\right\rangle=\left\langle h_{0}\right\rangle
, whence the second part of (a). Together with the equality
[F_{p}^{2},\widehat{t}_{1}\gamma]=1
, the results of
Table

5.3
lead to
[{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ}),{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})]=\left\langle h_{0}\right\rangle
, completing the missing part of (a).

The above implies that
{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})/\left\langle h_{0}\right\rangle
is abelian. We have
[Z_{1}.Z_{2},n]=[Z_{1}.Z_{2},\gamma_{1}]=[Z_{1}.Z_{2},n\gamma_{1}]=\left\langle h_{0}\right\rangle
.
Considering the action of
{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})
on
Z_{1}
and
Z_{2}
we obtain
{\mathrm{C}}_{{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})}(Z_{1})=\left\langle h_{0},F_{p}^{2},n\gamma_{1},\gamma_{1}F_{p}\right\rangle
and
{\mathrm{C}}_{{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})}(Z_{2})=\left\langle h_{0},F_{p},\gamma_{1}\right\rangle
. This leads to
{\mathrm{C}}_{{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})}(Z_{1}.Z_{2})=\left\langle h_{0},F_{p}\gamma_{1},F_{p}^{2}\right\rangle
and this group is abelian.
∎

Lemma
\@upn
5.19
— Comparison of
{\underline{E}}(M)
,
{\underline{E}}(G_{1})
and
{\underline{E}}(G_{2})
.

Assume
Hypothesis

5.15
. For
i=1,2
set
{\underline{E}}(G_{i}):=\left\langle\gamma_{i},F_{p}\right\rangle\leq\mathrm{Aut}(G_{i})
, and let
{{{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})}}\leq\mathrm{Aut}(G_{i})
be the subgroup of automorphisms of
G_{i}
induced by
{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})
. Then
{\underline{E}}(G_{2})={\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{2})
. Moreover,
{\underline{E}}(G_{1})
and
{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{1})
are
\widetilde{t}_{1}
-conjugate in
\mathrm{Out}(G_{1})
.

Proof.

Note that in our case
{{\nu F_{q}}}=\gamma F_{q}=F
as endomorphism of
{{\mathbf{G}}}
. Since
n=\widehat{t}_{1}n_{1}^{\circ}n_{2}^{\circ}
acts as
\gamma_{2}
on
G_{2}
, we see that
{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})
induces
\left\langle\gamma,F_{p}^{2}\right\rangle
on it. But the latter has same action on
{{{{\mathbf{G}}}^{F}}}
as
\left\langle\gamma,F_{p}\right\rangle
since
\gamma F_{q}
acts trivially and
2\nmid f
by
Remark

5.16
. Therefore
{\underline{E}}(G_{2})={\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{2})
, as claimed.
Via the isomorphism between
\mathrm{Out}({{{{\mathbf{G}}}^{F}}})
and
\operatorname{Z}({{\mathbf{G}}})_{F}\rtimes\left\langle\gamma,F_{p}\right\rangle
using
\mathcal{L}_{F}
and described in
Notation

2.14
, the group
{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{1})
corresponds to
\left\langle h_{0}\gamma,F_{p}^{2}\right\rangle
. Note that

\left\langle\gamma,F_{p}^{2}\right\rangle^{\mathbf{h}_{\underline{l_{1}}}(\varpi)}=\left\langle\gamma^{\mathbf{h}_{\underline{l_{1}}}(\varpi)},F_{p}^{2}\right\rangle=\left\langle h_{0}\gamma,F_{p}^{2}\right\rangle.

This shows that
{\underline{E}}(G_{1})
and
{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{1})
are
\widetilde{t}_{1}
-conjugate in
\mathrm{Out}(G_{1})
since
\mathbf{h}_{\underline{l_{1}}}(\varpi)=\mathcal{L}_{F}(\widetilde{t}_{1})
. ∎

5.C
The characters of
M
and their Clifford theory

The aim of the remainder of this chapter is the following statement, later used in the proof of Condition
\mathbf{A}(d)
and corresponding roughly to Theorem C of our Introduction. Recall that the groups
{\widetilde{T}}
from
Lemma

5.7
and
{\underline{E}}(M)
from
Definition

5.10
act on
M
. As expected we state that the actions of
\widetilde{T}M
and
{\underline{E}}(M)
on
M
are of “transversal nature”, and maximal extendibility holds with respect to
M\unlhd M{\underline{E}}(M)
for enough characters of
M
. In the case when
{\mathrm{C}}_{M{\underline{E}}(M)}(M)\neq\operatorname{Z}(M)
(i.e.
{\epsilon}=1
and
{\epsilon}_{1}=-1
) we provide a necessary refinement of that maximal extendibility for certain characters having
h_{0}
in their kernel.

Theorem
\@upn
5.20

Set
{{\widetilde{M}:=M\widetilde{T}}}
.

(a)

Every
\widetilde{M}
-orbit in
\mathrm{Irr}(M)
contains a character
\chi
such that

(i)

(\widetilde{M}{\underline{E}}(M))_{\chi}={\widetilde{M}}_{\chi}{{\underline{E}}(M)}_{\chi}
; and

(ii)

\chi
extends to
M{\underline{E}}(M)_{\chi}
.

Equivalently (see
[
S23a
, Lem. 2.4]
), there exists some
{\underline{E}}(M)
-stable
\widetilde{M}
-transversal
\mathbb{T}(M)
in
\mathrm{Irr}(M)
, such that maximal extendibility holds with respect to
M\unlhd M{\underline{E}}(M)
for
\mathbb{T}(M)
.

(b)

If
2\mid f
,
{\epsilon}=1
,
{\epsilon}_{1}=-1
,
\chi\in\mathbb{T}(M)
and
h_{0}\in\ker(\chi)
, then the extension of
\chi
can be chosen to have
v(\underline{F}_{p})^{f}
in its kernel, where
\underline{F}_{p}
is the image of
F_{p}
in
{\underline{E}}(M)
.

Recall

M_{0}=G_{1}.G_{2}\ \unlhd\ M^{\circ}=({{\mathbf{G}}}_{1}.{{\mathbf{G}}}_{2})^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}=M_{0}\left\langle\widehat{t}_{1}\widehat{t}_{2}\right\rangle\ \unlhd\ M=M^{\circ}\left\langle n\right\rangle,

with
M/M_{0}
a non-cyclic group of order 4, see
Lemma

5.6
.

The characters of
M
are studied through the characters of
M_{0}
, since
M_{0}=G_{1}.G_{2}
, with
G_{i}\cong\mathrm{D}_{l_{i},{\mathrm{sc}}}^{{\epsilon}_{i}}(q)
by
Definition

5.4
and therefore much information about
\mathrm{Irr}(G_{i})
is known from
Theorem

3.16
. Characters of
M
are obtained from those of
M_{0}
in the following way. Recall
\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}=\mathcal{L}_{{{\nu F_{q}}}}^{-1}(\operatorname{Z}({{\mathbf{G}}}))\cap{\mathbf{T}}
and
\widetilde{T}=({\mathbf{T}}\operatorname{Z}({{\mathbf{G}}}))^{{\nu F_{q}}}
from
Lemma

5.7
.

Lemma
\@upn
5.21

(a)

Let
{\phi\in\mathrm{Irr}(M_{0})}
,
{\widehat{\phi}\in\mathrm{Irr}(M^{\circ}_{\phi}\mid\phi)}
and
{\chi_{0}}\in\mathrm{Irr}(M^{\circ}_{\phi}\left\langle n\right\rangle_{\widehat{\phi}}\mid\widehat{\phi})
. If
M_{\phi}\neq M_{0}\left\langle\widehat{t}_{1}\widehat{t}_{2}n\right\rangle
,
then
\chi:=\chi_{0}^{M}
is irreducible.

(b)

Maximal extendibility holds with respect to
M_{0}\unlhd M_{0}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}
and
M_{0}\unlhd M_{0}\widetilde{T}
.

Note that every character
\phi\in\mathrm{Irr}(M_{0})
with
M_{\phi}=M_{0}\left\langle\widehat{t}_{1}\widehat{t}_{2}n\right\rangle
has the
\widecheck{T}
-conjugate character
\phi^{\prime}:=\phi^{\widetilde{t}_{1}\widetilde{t}_{2}}
. The character
\phi^{\prime}
then satisfies

M_{\phi^{\prime}}=(M_{\phi})^{\widetilde{t}_{1}\widetilde{t}_{2}}=M_{0}\left\langle\widehat{t}_{1}\widehat{t}_{2}n^{\widetilde{t}_{1}\widetilde{t}_{2}}\right\rangle=M_{0}\left\langle n\right\rangle,

since
[{\widetilde{t}_{1}\widetilde{t}_{2}},n]\in\widehat{t}_{1}\widehat{t}_{2}M_{0}
. Hence part (a) of the statement does not apply to
\phi
but applies to
\phi^{\prime}
.

Proof.

For part (a) note that the quotients
M^{\circ}/M_{0}
and
\left\langle n\right\rangle_{\widehat{\phi}}M^{\circ}_{\phi}/M^{\circ}_{\phi}
are cyclic, see
Lemma

5.6
. Therefore, the characters
\widehat{\phi}
and
\chi_{0}
are extensions of
\phi
according to
Proposition

2.2
(a). One has
M^{\circ}_{\phi}\left\langle n\right\rangle_{\widehat{\phi}}\leq M_{\widehat{\phi}}
. Now if
M^{\circ}_{\phi}\left\langle n\right\rangle_{\widehat{\phi}}=M_{\widehat{\phi}}
, then
\chi
is irreducible by Clifford theory (
2.1
). Clearly
M_{\widehat{\phi}}\leq M_{\phi}
.
Recall that
M/M_{0}
is a Klein
4
-group and the subgroups of
M
containing
M_{0}
are
M_{0}
,
M_{0}\left\langle n\right\rangle
,
M_{0}\left\langle\widehat{t}_{1}\widehat{t}_{2}n\right\rangle
,
M^{\circ}
and
M
. The group
M_{\phi}
is one of those groups. Whenever
M_{\phi}\geq M^{\circ}
or
M_{\phi}=M_{0}
the above construction leads to
M_{\widehat{\phi}}=M^{\circ}_{\phi}\left\langle n\right\rangle_{\widehat{\phi}}
. Otherwise
M_{\phi}=M_{0}\left\langle n\widehat{t}_{1}\widehat{t}_{2}\right\rangle
, and hence
M_{\widehat{\phi}}\neq M^{\circ}_{\phi}\left\langle n\right\rangle_{\widehat{\phi}}
in this case. We see that
\chi
is irreducible unless
M_{\phi}=M_{0}\left\langle\widehat{t}_{1}\widehat{t}_{2}n\right\rangle
.

Next, we consider the claim in part (b). Recall the definitions of
\widehat{t}_{1},\widehat{t}_{2},\widetilde{t}_{1}
and
\widetilde{t}_{2}
from
Lemma

5.3
and
Lemma

5.7
. Set
{{{\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{278933}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i}}}:=\left\langle\widehat{t}_{i},\widetilde{t}_{i},G_{i}\right\rangle
. When
l_{i}\geq 2
this is consistent with
Notation

2.15
since
\mathcal{L}_{{{\nu F_{q}}}}(\left\langle\widehat{t}_{i},\widetilde{t}_{i}\right\rangle)=\operatorname{Z}({{\mathbf{G}}}_{i})
, while when
l_{i}=1
then
{\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{278933}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i}
is abelian. So
Lemma

3.19
implies that maximal extendibility holds with respect to
G_{i}\unlhd{\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{278933}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i}
. On the other hand, the group
\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}
of

satisfies

\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}\leq{\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{278933}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{1}.{\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{278933}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{2}

since
\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}=\left\langle\widetilde{t}_{1}\widetilde{t}_{2},\widehat{t}_{1},\widehat{t}_{2}\right\rangle(T\cap M_{0})
by

. Hence, maximal extendibility holds with respect to

and

. As

is abelian, this implies maximal extendibility with respect to

, see
Proposition

2.2
(b).

Since
\widetilde{T}=({\mathbf{T}}\operatorname{Z}({\widetilde{\mathbf{G}}}))^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}\leq\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}\operatorname{Z}({\widetilde{\mathbf{G}}})
, we also get maximal extendibility with respect to

and with respect to

.
∎

Recall
M^{\circ}=({{\mathbf{G}}}_{1}.{{\mathbf{G}}}_{2})^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}=M_{0}T
for
{T}:={\mathbf{T}}^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}
, and

{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ}):={\underline{E}}(M)\left\langle n,h_{0}\right\rangle.

Proposition
\@upn
5.22

Let
\phi\in\mathrm{Irr}(M_{0})
and
\chi\in\mathrm{Irr}(M\mid\phi)
. If
{\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{\phi}\leq T\left\langle\widehat{t}_{1}\right\rangle

, then

where

.

Proof.

and that

stabilizes

according to

(c) and
