G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i}))_{\chi}\neq{({{\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{278933}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i}})}_{\chi}{{{\underline{E}}}_{M}({\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{278933}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i})}_{\chi}\right.\right\},

so that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Irr}(G_{i})=\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{i}\sqcup{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\sqcup\mathbb{D}_{i}.

We also define
{{{{\mathbb{E}}^{\prime}_{i}}}}=\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{i}\cap\big(\bigcup_{x\in{\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.63779pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.63779pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.63779pt$\hss\resizebox{201988}{124882}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.63779pt$\hss\resizebox{144277}{89202}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i}}{}^{x}{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\big)
and
{{{\mathbb{D}}^{\prime}_{i}}}=\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{i}\cap\big(\bigcup_{x\in{\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.63779pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.63779pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.63779pt$\hss\resizebox{201988}{124882}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.63779pt$\hss\resizebox{144277}{89202}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i}}{}^{x}\mathbb{D}_{i}\big)
.

The set
{{\mathbb{E}}^{\prime}_{i}}
is the subset of all characters in
\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{i}
, which are
{\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{278933}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i}
-conjugate to an element of
{\mathbb{E}}_{i}
. The set
{\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{i}}
is the subset of all characters in
\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{i}
, which are
{\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{278933}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i}
-conjugate to an element of
\mathbb{D}_{i}
. The definitions of the above character sets are analogous to those of
Definition

3.9
(and
Notation

3.15
). Accordingly, the characters satisfy the following statement.

Proposition
\@upn
5.25

Let
i\in\{1,2\}
. Then the character sets of
Definition

5.24
satisfy:

(a)

\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{i}
contains some
\widetilde{T}
-transversal in
\mathrm{Irr}(G_{i})
.

(b)

If
\chi\in{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\cup{\mathbb{E}}^{\prime}_{i}
, then
h_{0}\in\ker(\chi)
,
({\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{278933}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i})_{\chi}=G_{i}\left\langle\widehat{t}_{i}\right\rangle
,
\chi^{n}=\chi
and
\chi
extends to
G_{i}\left\langle\widehat{t}_{i},\gamma_{i}\right\rangle
.

(c)

If
\chi\in\mathbb{D}_{i}\cup\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{i}
, then
h_{0}\in\ker(\chi)
,
({\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{278933}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i})_{\chi}=G_{i}
and every
\widehat{\chi}\in\mathrm{Irr}(\left\langle G_{i},\widehat{t}_{i}\right\rangle\mid\chi)
is
n
-invariant. More precisely
\chi^{n}=\chi
if
\chi\in\mathbb{D}_{i}^{\prime}
and
\chi^{n\widehat{t}_{i}}=\chi
if
\chi\in\mathbb{D}_{i}
.

Moreover,
{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})_{\chi}
is cyclic whenever
\chi\in\mathbb{D}_{i}
. If
\chi\in\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{i}
, then
(M{\underline{E}}(M))_{\chi}\leq M_{0}{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})_{\chi}
.

(d)

If
|Z_{i}|=2
, then
\mathbb{D}_{i}={\mathbb{E}}_{i}=\emptyset
and
\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{i}=\mathrm{Irr}(G_{i})
.

(e)

If
{\epsilon}_{i}=-1
, then
{\mathbb{E}}_{i}=\emptyset
. If
2\nmid f
, then
{\mathbb{E}}_{1}={\mathbb{E}}_{2}=\emptyset
.

(f)

If
\chi\in\mathrm{Irr}(G_{i})\setminus{\mathbb{E}}_{i}
, then
\chi
extends to
G_{i}{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})_{\chi}
. If
\chi\in{\mathbb{E}}_{i}
, then
\chi
extends to
G_{i}E^{\prime\prime}
for any
E^{\prime\prime}\leq{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})_{\chi}
with an even index
|{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})_{\chi}/E^{\prime\prime}|
.

Proof.

The groups
{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})
and
{\underline{E}}(G_{i})
induce the same subgroup of
\mathrm{Aut}(G_{i})
for every
i\in\{1,2\}
unless
i=2
and
Hypothesis

5.15
holds, see
Lemma

5.14
and
Lemma

5.19
. In those cases, the sets obtained in
Definition

3.9
(and
Notation

3.15
) for
\mathrm{Irr}(G_{i})
coincide with the present
\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{i}
,
{\mathbb{E}}_{i}
,
\mathbb{D}_{i}
via the isomorphism between
G_{i}
and
\mathrm{D}^{{\epsilon}_{i}}_{l_{i},\text{sc}}(q)
from
Definition

5.4
. If
Hypothesis

5.15
holds, then
{\underline{E}}(G_{1})
and
{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{1})
are
\widetilde{t}_{1}
-conjugate in
\mathrm{Out}(G_{1})
, so the sets from
Definition

3.9
for
\mathrm{Irr}(G_{i})
are
\widetilde{t}_{1}
-conjugate to the ones from
Definition

5.24
.
Recall that in the case of (b) we can assume that
{\epsilon}_{1}={\epsilon}_{2}=1
and hence
n
acts as
\gamma_{i}
on
G_{i}
. In case of part (c),
n
acts either as
\gamma_{i}
or
\gamma_{i}\widehat{t}_{i}
, but it always corresponds to an automorphism of
{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})
by construction.
This implies that parts (a), (b) and the two first sentences of (c) follow from
Theorem

3.16
and
Lemma

3.12
(a).

We now show that
{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})_{\chi}
is cyclic whenever
\chi\in\mathbb{D}_{i}
.
Let
\chi^{\prime}\in\mathbb{D}_{i}^{\prime}
be in the
{\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{278933}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i}
-orbit of
\chi
. Recall that this
{\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{278933}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i}
-orbit is of length
4
. Via the identification

{\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{278933}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i}{\underline{E}}_{M}({\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{278933}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i})/(G_{i}{\mathrm{C}}_{{\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.63779pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.63779pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.63779pt$\hss\resizebox{201988}{124882}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.63779pt$\hss\resizebox{144277}{89202}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i}{\underline{E}}_{M}({\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.63779pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.63779pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.63779pt$\hss\resizebox{201988}{124882}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.63779pt$\hss\resizebox{144277}{89202}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i})}(G_{i}))\cong\operatorname{Z}({{{{\mathbf{G}}}^{F}}})\rtimes{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i}),

the group
({\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{278933}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i}{\underline{E}}_{M}({\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{278933}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i}))_{\chi^{\prime}}
then corresponds to a subgroup of
{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})
and
({\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{278933}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i}{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i}))_{\chi}
corresponds to a subgroup of the form
{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})^{z}
for some
z\in\operatorname{Z}({{{{\mathbf{G}}}^{F}}})\setminus\left\langle h_{0}\right\rangle=\operatorname{Z}({{{{\mathbf{G}}}^{F}}})\setminus{\mathrm{C}}_{\operatorname{Z}({{{{\mathbf{G}}}^{F}}})}({\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i}))
. The group
{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})_{\chi}
corresponds then to a subgroup of
{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})\cap{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})^{z}
which is cyclic by a straight-forward discussion.

If
\chi\in\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{i}\subseteq\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{i}
, then the inclusion
(M{\underline{E}}(M))_{\chi}=(M^{\circ}{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ}))_{\chi}\leq M_{0}{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})_{\chi}
follows from the fact that
M^{\circ}
acts by diagonal automorphisms on
G_{i}
and
{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})
acts by
{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})
on
G_{i}
. Recall that
\chi
is in this situation not stable under any non-inner diagonal automorphism.

For the proof of (d), assume
|Z_{i}|=2
. Then
Z_{i}{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})
coincides with
Z_{i}\left\langle\gamma_{i},F_{p}\right\rangle
and is abelian. Then (a) implies
\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{i}=\mathrm{Irr}(G_{i})
and
\mathbb{D}_{i}={\mathbb{E}}_{i}=\emptyset
as also explained in (4) of the proof of
Theorem

3.16
.

For part (e), assume first
Hypothesis

5.11
. Then
{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})
acts by
\left\langle\gamma_{i},\left.F_{p}\right\rceil_{{{{\mathbf{G}}}^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}}}\right\rangle
, which is cyclic whenever
2\nmid f
or
{\epsilon}_{i}=-1
. This then implies that every character of
G_{i}
extends to its stabilizer in
G_{i}{\underline{E}}_{M}({G}_{i})
by
Proposition

2.2
(a), and we get
{\mathbb{E}}_{i}=\emptyset
. This is (e) in that case.

Assume now that
Hypothesis

5.15
holds and therefore
2\nmid f
. Then
{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{1})=\left\langle\widehat{t}_{1}\gamma,F_{p}^{2}\right\rangle
and
{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{2})=\left\langle\gamma_{2},F_{p}\right\rangle
both act cyclically on
{{{\mathbf{G}}}^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}}
. Then
{\mathbb{E}}_{1}={\mathbb{E}}_{2}=\emptyset
again as above.

Part (f) is clear for
\chi\in\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{i}
. If
\chi\in\mathbb{D}_{i}
the group
{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})_{\chi}
is cyclic by part (c). Otherwise if
\chi\in{\mathbb{E}}_{i}
, then the group
E^{\prime\prime}
is either cyclic or contains
\gamma_{i}
since
{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})/\left\langle\gamma\right\rangle
is cyclic. In the latter case, let
F^{\prime}\in{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})_{\chi}
be such that
{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})_{\chi}=\left\langle F^{\prime},\gamma_{i}\right\rangle
and
E^{\prime\prime}\leq\left\langle(F^{\prime})^{2},\gamma_{i}\right\rangle
. Every extension
\widetilde{\chi}
of
\chi
to
G_{i}\left\langle\gamma_{i}\right\rangle
is not
F^{\prime}
-invariant by the definition of
{\mathbb{E}}_{i}
, but since
F^{\prime}
can only permute the two extensions of
\chi
we see that
\widetilde{\chi}
is
(F^{\prime})^{2}
-invariant and hence
\chi
extends to
G_{i}E^{\prime\prime}
.
∎

Because of the central product structure
M_{0}=G_{1}.G_{2}
with
G_{1}\cap G_{2}=\left\langle h_{0}\right\rangle
(see
Lemma

5.2
), any subsets
\mathbb{G}_{1}\subseteq\mathrm{Irr}(G_{1})
and
\mathbb{G}_{2}\subseteq\mathrm{Irr}(G_{2})
define

{{\mathbb{G}_{1}.\mathbb{G}_{2}}}:=\{\chi_{1}.\chi_{2}\mid\chi_{i}\in\mathbb{G}_{i}\text{ with }\mathrm{Irr}(\left.\chi_{1}\right\rceil_{\left\langle h_{0}\right\rangle})=\mathrm{Irr}(\left.\chi_{2}\right\rceil_{\left\langle h_{0}\right\rangle})\}\subseteq\mathrm{Irr}(M_{0}).

Recall
{\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}:=\mathcal{L}^{-1}_{{{\nu F_{q}}}}(\operatorname{Z}({{\mathbf{G}}}))\cap{\mathbf{T}}
and
{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})={\underline{E}}(M)\left\langle n,h_{0}\right\rangle
by the definition in
Lemma

5.13
and
Lemma

5.18
. The following statement helps us split the proof of
Theorem

5.20
into cases.

Proposition
\@upn
5.26
— Properties of
\widetilde{T}
-orbits in
\mathrm{Irr}(M_{0})
.

Let
\phi^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Irr}(M_{0})
. There exists some
t\in\widetilde{T}
such that
\phi=(\phi^{\prime})^{t}
satisfies one of the following properties:

(i)

\phi\in\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{1}.\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{2}
; or

(ii)

\phi\in{{\mathbb{E}}^{\prime}}_{1}.{\mathbb{E}}_{2}\sqcup\mathbb{D}_{1}^{\prime}.\mathbb{D}_{2}\sqcup\mathbb{D}_{1}^{\prime}.{\mathbb{E}}_{2}\sqcup{\mathbb{E}}_{1}.\mathbb{D}_{2}^{\prime}
with
\{|Z_{1}|,|Z_{2}|\}=\{4\}
.

Proof.

Recall that the action of
\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}
on
{{{\mathbf{G}}}^{{{\nu F_{q}}}}}
coincides with the action of
\widetilde{T}
. Set
\phi^{\prime}_{i}\in\mathrm{Irr}(G_{i})
such that
\phi^{\prime}=\phi^{\prime}_{1}.\phi^{\prime}_{2}
. Recall
\mathrm{Irr}(G_{i})=\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{i}\sqcup{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\sqcup\mathbb{D}_{i}
, see
Definition

5.24
, with
{\mathbb{E}}_{i}=\mathbb{D}_{i}=\emptyset
whenever
|Z_{i}|\neq 4
as recalled in the preceding proof.

Assume
\phi_{1}^{\prime}
and all its
\widetilde{T}
-conjugates belong to
\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{1}
. In this case,
Proposition

5.25
(a) allows us to choose
x\in\widetilde{T}
so that
(\phi^{\prime}_{2})^{x}\in\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{2}
additionally, and therefore
{\phi_{1}.\phi_{2}}:=(\phi^{\prime}_{1}.\phi^{\prime}_{2})^{x}\in\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{1}.\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{2}
, as claimed in (i).

If we are not in case (i), we see then that there is a
\widetilde{T}
-conjugate
(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})
of
(\phi_{1}^{\prime},\phi_{2}^{\prime})
in
({\mathbb{E}}^{\prime}_{1}\sqcup\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{1})\times({\mathbb{E}}_{2}\sqcup\mathbb{D}_{2})
, also forcing
|Z_{1}|=|Z_{2}|=4
. This gives (ii) up to the case
(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})\in{\mathbb{E}}_{1}^{\prime}\times\mathbb{D}_{2}
.

Proposition

5.25
implies
{\mathbb{E}}_{1}^{\prime}={\mathbb{E}}_{1}^{\widetilde{t}_{1}}
and
\mathbb{D}_{2}^{\widetilde{t}_{2}}=\mathbb{D}_{2}^{\prime}
. Hence if
\phi_{1}.\phi_{2}\in{\mathbb{E}}_{1}^{\prime}.\mathbb{D}_{2}
, a
\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}
-conjugate of
\phi_{1}.\phi_{2}
is contained in
{\mathbb{E}}_{1}.\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{2}
. But this is also a
\widetilde{T}
-conjugate, so we are in the case (ii).
∎

The above case (i) implies a familiar transversality of stabilizers.

Lemma
\@upn
5.27

Every
\phi\in\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{1}.\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{2}
satisfies
(\widetilde{T}{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ}))_{\phi}={\widetilde{T}}_{\phi}{{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})}_{\phi}
.

Proof.

For
i\in\underline{2}
, let
\phi_{i}\in\mathrm{Irr}(G_{i})
with
\phi=\phi_{1}.\phi_{2}
.
Recall that
\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{i}
was defined using
{\underline{E}}_{M}({\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{278933}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i})
which acts as
{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})
on
G_{i}
, hence
\phi_{i}\in\mathrm{Irr}(\left.\phi\right\rceil_{G_{i}})
satisfies
({\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{278933}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i}{\underline{E}}_{M}({\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{278933}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i}))_{\phi_{i}}={({\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{278933}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i})}_{\phi_{i}}{{\underline{E}}_{M}({\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{278933}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i})}_{\phi_{i}}
.

In order to verify the statement, we have to prove that for every
\widetilde{g}\in\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}
and
e\in{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})
with
\phi^{\widetilde{g}e}=\phi
, one has
\phi^{\widetilde{g}}=\phi^{e}=\phi
in the first place.

Since
\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}\leq T\left\langle\widetilde{t}_{1},\widetilde{t}_{2},\widehat{t}_{1},\widehat{t}_{2}\right\rangle
and
{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})
acts as
{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})
on
G_{i}
, one may find
\widetilde{g}_{i}\in{\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{278933}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i}
,
e_{i}\in{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})
such that
\widetilde{g}=\widetilde{g}_{1}\widetilde{g}_{2}
and
e
acts as
e_{1}e_{2}
on
M_{0}
. Then
(\phi_{1}\phi_{2})^{\widetilde{g}e}=\phi_{1}\phi_{2}
implies
\phi_{i}^{\widetilde{g}_{i}e_{i}}=\phi_{i}
for
i=1,2
by restriction.
From the definition of the sets
\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{i}
we now get
\phi_{i}^{\widetilde{g}_{i}}=\phi_{i}^{e_{i}}=\phi_{i}
and therefore our claim that
\phi^{\widetilde{g}}=\phi=\phi^{e_{1}e_{2}}=\phi^{e}
.
∎

Proposition

5.26
above clearly leads to checking
Theorem

5.20
by considering successively
\widetilde{T}
-orbits in
\mathrm{Irr}(M)
containing an element in
\mathrm{Irr}(M\mid\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{1}.\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{2})
,
\mathrm{Irr}(M\mid{\mathbb{E}}^{\prime}_{1}.{\mathbb{E}}_{2})
or
\mathrm{Irr}(M\mid\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{1}.\mathbb{D}_{2}\sqcup\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{1}.{\mathbb{E}}_{2}\sqcup{\mathbb{E}}_{1}.\mathbb{D}_{2}^{\prime})
, see the next two sections.

Beforehand we have nevertheless to prove a statement about maximal extendibility. Recall
{\widetilde{M}:=M\widetilde{T}}
.

Proposition
\@upn
5.28

Maximal extendibility holds with respect to
M\unlhd\widetilde{M}=\widetilde{T}M
.

Proof.

Let
\chi\in\mathrm{Irr}(M)
. Assume first that
{\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{\chi}/T
is cyclic. Then since
\widetilde{T}\leq\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}\operatorname{Z}({\widetilde{\mathbf{G}}})
by the definition of
\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}
in
Lemma

5.7
and therefore
\widetilde{M}\leq\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{439166}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{365185}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}\operatorname{Z}({\widetilde{\mathbf{G}}})
, we have that
\widetilde{M}_{\chi}/M(\widetilde{M}\cap\operatorname{Z}({\widetilde{\mathbf{G}}}))
is cyclic. Then
\chi
extends to
\widetilde{M}_{\chi}
by
Proposition

2.2
(a).

We now assume that
{\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{\chi}/T
is not cyclic. Then
{\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{\chi}=\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}
and
we can assume
\chi^{\widetilde{t}_{1}\widetilde{t}_{2}}=\chi^{\widehat{t}_{1}}=\chi
. If
\phi\in\mathrm{Irr}(\left.\chi\right\rceil_{M_{0}})
, then
\phi^{\widetilde{t}_{1}\widetilde{t}_{2}}
is in the
M
-orbit of
\phi
since
\phi^{\widetilde{t}_{1}\widetilde{t}_{2}}
also belongs to
\mathrm{Irr}(\left.\chi\right\rceil_{M_{0}})
. For
\phi_{i}\in\mathrm{Irr}(G_{i})
with
\phi=\phi_{1}.\phi_{2}
, we see that the
M
-orbit of
\phi_{i}
is
\widetilde{t}_{i}
-stable.
Therefore, the
\left\langle n,\widehat{t}_{i}\right\rangle
-orbit of
\phi_{i}
is
\widetilde{t}_{i}
-stable. On the other hand,
Proposition

5.25
(b) and (c) implies that every
\left\langle n,\widehat{t}_{i}\right\rangle
-orbit containing some
\psi^{\prime}\in\mathbb{D}_{i}\sqcup\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{i}\sqcup{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\sqcup{\mathbb{E}}^{\prime}_{i}
is not
\widetilde{t}_{i}
-stable. Accordingly
\phi_{i}\in\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{i}\setminus({\mathbb{E}}^{\prime}_{i}\cup\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{i})
. Taken together, we see that
\phi\in\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{1}.\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{2}
and therefore

(\widetilde{T}{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ}))_{\phi}={\widetilde{T}}_{\phi}{{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})}_{\phi}

by
Lemma

5.27
. In particular
M_{\phi}\neq M_{0}\left\langle\widehat{t}_{1}\widehat{t}_{2}n\right\rangle
and
Lemma

5.21
(a) then ensures that
\chi=\widetilde{\phi}^{M}
, where
\widetilde{\phi}
is an extension of
\phi
to
M_{\widehat{\phi}}
for some extension
\widehat{\phi}\in\mathrm{Irr}(M^{\circ}_{\phi})
of
\phi
.
Lemma

5.21
(b) tells us that maximal extendibility holds with respect to
M_{0}\lhd M_{0}\widetilde{T}
, so the character
\widetilde{\phi}
extends to
M_{0}\widetilde{T}_{\phi}
. According to
Proposition

2.2
(e), there exists an extension
\psi
of
\widetilde{\phi}
to
\widetilde{T}_{\widetilde{\phi}}M_{\widehat{\phi}}
. The character
\psi^{M\widetilde{T}_{\widehat{\phi}}}
is by this construction an extension of
\chi
to
M\widetilde{T}_{\chi}
as required. ∎

5.E
Proof of
Theorem

5.20
. Characters in
\mathrm{Irr}(M\mid\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{1}.\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{2})

In this section we begin the verification of
Theorem

5.20
through studying
\mathrm{Irr}(M)
as a union of sets
\mathrm{Irr}(M\mid\mathcal{X})
for the various subsets
\mathcal{X}\subseteq\mathrm{Irr}(M_{0})
singled out in
Proposition

5.26
. Here we start with
\mathrm{Irr}(M\mid\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{1}.\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{2})
.

Recall
\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}:=\mathcal{L}_{{{\nu F_{q}}}}^{-1}(\operatorname{Z}({{\mathbf{G}}}))\cap{\mathbf{T}}
,
{\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{439166}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{365185}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}^{\circ}:=\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}M^{\circ}
,

{{{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})}}:={\underline{E}}(M)\left\langle n,h_{0}\right\rangle
,
{\underline{E}}({\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{439166}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{365185}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}^{\circ}):={\underline{E}}(\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{439166}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{365185}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits})\left\langle n,h_{0}\right\rangle
,
{{\widehat{M}:=M{\underline{E}}(M)}}
and
{{\widehat{M}_{0}}}:=M_{0}{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})
.

In our situation,
Lemma

5.27
leads to the following.

Lemma
\@upn
5.29

Let
\phi\in\mathrm{Irr}(M_{0})
and
\widehat{\phi}\in\mathrm{Irr}(M^{\circ}_{\phi}\mid\phi)
. Assume:

(i)

\phi
satisfies
(\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\underline{E}}({\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{439166}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{365185}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}^{\circ}))_{\phi}={\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{\phi}{{\underline{E}}({\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{439166}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{365185}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}^{\circ})}_{\phi}
or equivalently
(\widehat{M}_{0}\widetilde{T})_{\phi}={(\widehat{M}_{0})}_{\phi}{\widetilde{T}}_{\phi}
;

(ii)

\phi_{i}\in\mathrm{Irr}(\left.\phi\right\rceil_{G_{i}})
extends to
M_{0}{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})_{\phi_{i}}
for every
i\in\{1,2\}
.

Then:

(a)

Every
\chi\in\mathrm{Irr}(M\mid\phi)
satisfies
(\widetilde{M}\widehat{M})_{\chi}=\widetilde{M}_{\chi}\widehat{M}_{\chi}
and extends to
\widehat{M}_{\chi}
.

(b)

Every
\kappa\in\mathrm{Irr}(M^{\circ}\mid\phi)
satisfies
(\widetilde{M}\widehat{M})_{\kappa}=\widetilde{M}_{\kappa}\widehat{M}_{\kappa}
and extends to
\widehat{M}_{\kappa}
.

(c)

If in addition
{\epsilon}_{1}={\epsilon}_{2}=-1
(or equivalently
v\in{{\mathbf{G}}}\setminus\{1_{{\mathbf{G}}}\}
) and
2\mid f
(hence
\{|Z_{1}|,|Z_{2}|\}=\{2\}
according to
2.24
(c)), then every
\chi\in\mathrm{Irr}(M\mid\phi)
with
h_{0}\in\ker(\chi)
has an extension
\widetilde{\chi}
to
\widehat{M}_{\chi}=M{\underline{E}}(M)_{\chi}
with
vF_{q}\in\ker(\widetilde{\chi})
.

Proof.

We apply
Lemma

2.4
with
A:=\widetilde{T}\widehat{M}
,
X=M_{0}
,
\widetilde{X}:=\widetilde{T}M_{0}
,
Y:=\widehat{M}_{0}
and
L:=M
and
\phi
as the character. We know that maximal extendibility holds with respect to
M_{0}\unlhd M_{0}\widetilde{T}
according to
Lemma

5.21
(b) and therefore
\phi
extends to
M_{0}\widetilde{T}_{\phi}
.

The quotient
\widetilde{T}M_{0}/M_{0}=\widetilde{T}/(\widetilde{T}\cap M_{0})
is abelian. The assumption (ii) implies that
\phi
extends to
(\widehat{M}_{0})_{{\widehat{\phi}}}
for
\widehat{\phi}\in\mathrm{Irr}(M^{\circ}_{\phi}\mid\phi)
, see
Proposition

5.23
. The group
\widehat{M}_{0}/M_{0}=(M_{0}{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ}))/M_{0}
is isomorphic to a subgroup of
{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})/({\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})\cap M^{\circ})
and
{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})/({\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})\cap M^{\circ})
is abelian as
[{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ}),{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})]\leq M^{\circ}
according to
Lemma

5.13
and
Lemma

5.18
, respectively.

In combination with the assumption (i), we see that all assumptions of
Lemma

2.4
are satisfied and the statements in (a) and (b) follow then from (b) and (c) of
Lemma

2.4
.

It remains to consider the case where
2\mid f
,
{\epsilon}_{1}={\epsilon}_{2}=-1
and
h_{0}\in\ker(\chi)
. As seen before,
Proposition

5.23
(b) tells us that
\phi
has an extension to
(\widehat{M}_{0})_{{\widehat{\phi}}}
with
\left\langle vF_{q}\right\rangle
in its kernel. Then the statement in (c) follows from
Lemma

2.4
(d) with
z=vF_{q}
.
∎

In a first application of this statement we verify
Theorem

5.20
for
\widetilde{M}
-orbits containing some character in
\mathrm{Irr}(M\mid\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{1}.\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{2})
. Part (b) of the following statement is useful for the proof of Condition
\mathbf{B}(d)
.

Proposition
\@upn
5.30
— Stabilizer and extensions of
\mathrm{Irr}(M\mid\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{1}.\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{2})
.

Let
\phi\in\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{1}.\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{2}
.

(a)

Then every character
\chi\in\mathrm{Irr}(M\mid\phi)
satisfies

(i)

(\widetilde{M}\widehat{M})_{\chi}={\widetilde{M}}_{\chi}{\widehat{M}}_{\chi}
; and

(ii)

\chi
extends to some
\widetilde{\chi}\in\mathrm{Irr}(\widehat{M}_{\chi})
such that
vF_{q}\in\ker(\widetilde{\chi})
whenever
v\in{{\mathbf{G}}}\setminus\{1_{{\mathbf{G}}}\}
and
h_{0}\in\ker(\chi)
.

(b)

Maximal extendibility holds with respect to
M^{\circ}\unlhd\widehat{M}=M{\underline{E}}(M)
for
\mathrm{Irr}(M^{\circ}\mid\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{1}.\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{2})
.

(c)

If
2\mid f
,
{\epsilon}_{1}={\epsilon}_{2}=-1
and
\chi\in\mathrm{Irr}(M\mid\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{1}.\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{2})
with
h_{0}\in\ker(\chi)
, then
\chi
extends to
M{\underline{E}}(M)_{\chi}/\left\langle{{\nu F_{q}}}\right\rangle
.

Proof.

Recall
{\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{278933}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i}:=\left\langle G_{i},\widehat{t}_{i},\widetilde{t}_{i}\right\rangle
and
{\underline{E}}_{M}({\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{278933}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i})
is the subgroup of
\mathrm{Aut}({\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{326422}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{278933}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{i})
induced by
{\underline{E}}({\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{565795}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{439166}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{365185}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle M\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}^{\circ})
, see
Definition

5.10
.
According to
Lemma

5.27
,
(\widehat{M}_{0}\widetilde{T})_{\phi}={(\widehat{M}_{0})}_{\phi}{\widetilde{T}}_{\phi}
. Let

be such that

. By the definition of

the character
\phi_{i}
satisfies
({\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{412222}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle G\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern and extends to

.

Hence the assumptions from

5.F
Proof of
Theorem

5.20
. Above the other characters of
M_{0}

In this section we study
\widetilde{M}
-orbits
{\mathcal{O}}
in
\mathrm{Irr}(M)
with
{\mathcal{O}}\cap\mathrm{Irr}(M\mid\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{1}.\overline{\mathbb{T}}_{2})=\emptyset
. According to
Proposition

5.26
,
{\mathcal{O}}
then contains a character
\chi^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Irr}(M\mid\phi)
for some
\phi\in{\mathbb{E}}^{\prime}_{1}.{\mathbb{E}}_{2}\sqcup\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{1}.\mathbb{D}_{2}\sqcup\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{1}.{\mathbb{E}}_{2}\sqcup{\mathbb{E}}_{1}.\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{2}
, while
|Z_{1}|=|Z_{2}|=4
.

Proposition
\@upn
5.31

Assume
|Z_{1}|=|Z_{2}|=4
. Let
\phi\in{\mathbb{E}}^{\prime}_{1}.{\mathbb{E}}_{2}\sqcup\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{1}.\mathbb{D}_{2}\sqcup\mathbb{D}_{1}^{\prime}.{\mathbb{E}}_{2}\sqcup{\mathbb{E}}_{1}.\mathbb{D}_{2}^{\prime}
. Then every
\chi\in\mathrm{Irr}(M\mid\phi)
satisfies

(i)

(\widetilde{M}\widehat{M})_{\chi}={\widetilde{M}}_{\chi}{\widehat{M}}_{\chi}
and

(ii)

\chi
extends to
\widehat{M}_{\chi}=M{\underline{E}}(M)_{\chi}
.

Proof.

Set
\phi=\phi_{1}\phi_{2}
with
\phi_{i}\in\mathrm{Irr}(G_{i})
for
i=1,2
. We are going to prove the following points for at least one
\chi\in\mathrm{Irr}(M\mid\phi)
.

(a)

\chi^{\widehat{t}_{1}}=\chi
;

(b)

M_{\phi}\in\{M_{0},M_{0}\left\langle n\right\rangle,M\}
and
\phi
extends to some
\chi_{0}\in\mathrm{Irr}(M_{\phi}\mid\phi)
, and

(c)

if
\phi_{i}\in{\mathbb{E}}_{i}
and we denote by
E_{i}^{\prime}\leq\mathrm{Aut}(G_{i})
the subgroup induced by
{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})_{\chi_{0}}
, then the index
|{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})_{\phi_{i}}/E^{\prime}_{i}|
is even.

(d)

(M{\underline{E}}(M))_{\phi}\leq M_{0}{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})
or
M_{\phi}=M
.

Let us see first how those points imply our Proposition.
Since
\widetilde{M}\widehat{M}
acts trivially on
M/M_{0}
it suffices to prove the points (i) and (ii) for some
\chi\in\mathrm{Irr}(M\mid\phi)
to have them for all. Set
T_{i}:=T\cap G_{i}
. The assumption that
\phi=\phi_{1}\phi_{2}
belongs to
{\mathbb{E}}^{\prime}_{1}.{\mathbb{E}}_{2}\sqcup\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{1}.\mathbb{D}_{2}\sqcup\mathbb{D}_{1}^{\prime}.{\mathbb{E}}_{2}\sqcup{\mathbb{E}}_{1}.\mathbb{D}_{2}^{\prime}
implies
\phi_{i}\in\mathbb{D}_{i}\cup\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{i}\cup{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\cup{\mathbb{E}}^{\prime}_{i}
. According to
Proposition

5.25
(b) and (c) the inclusion
T_{i}\left\langle\widehat{t}_{i},\widetilde{t}_{i}\right\rangle_{\phi_{i}}\leq T_{i}\left\langle\widehat{t}_{i}\right\rangle
holds and
h_{0}\in\ker(\phi)
. This implies
{\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}_{\phi}\leq T\left\langle\widehat{t}_{1}\right\rangle
since
\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{379200}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{304069}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{263696}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle T\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}=(T_{1}.T_{2})\left\langle\widehat{t}_{1},\widehat{t}_{2},\widetilde{t}_{1}\widetilde{t}_{2}\right\rangle
according to
Lemma

5.7
.
Then (1) implies
(\widetilde{M}\widehat{M})_{\chi}={\widetilde{M}}_{\chi}{\widehat{M}}_{\chi}
according to
Proposition

5.22
.

By (2),
M_{\phi}\in\{M_{0}\left\langle n\right\rangle_{\phi},M\}
and
\chi=\chi_{0}^{M}
for some extension
\chi_{0}\in\mathrm{Irr}(M_{\phi}\mid\phi)
of
\phi
, see also
Lemma

5.21
(a). Let
E_{i}^{\prime}\leq\mathrm{Aut}(G_{i})
be induced by
{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})_{\chi_{0}}
. Note that
E_{i}^{\prime}\leq{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})_{\phi_{i}}
, and
\phi_{i}\in\mathrm{Irr}(G_{i})
extends to
G_{i}{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})_{\phi_{i}}
according to
Proposition

5.25
(f), whenever
\phi_{i}\in\mathrm{Irr}(G_{i})\setminus{\mathbb{E}}_{i}
. If
i\in\{1,2\}
with
\phi_{i}\in{\mathbb{E}}_{i}
, then by (3) the group
E_{i}^{\prime}
has even index in
{\underline{E}}_{M}(G_{i})_{\phi_{i}}
and
\phi_{i}
extends to
G_{i}E_{i}^{\prime}
according to
Proposition

5.25
(f). Hence
\phi
extends to
M_{0}E(M^{\circ})_{\chi_{0}}
according to
Proposition

5.23
.

Moreover
(M{\underline{E}}(M))_{\chi_{0}}\leq M_{\chi_{0}}{\underline{E}}(M^{\circ})_{\chi_{0}}
follows from (4), since
(M{\underline{E}}(M))_{\chi_{0}}=M{\underline{E}}(M)_{\chi_{0}}
in case
\chi_{0}
is an extension of
\phi
to
M
, or

. Then this implies that
\chi_{0}
extends to

, according to

2.2
(e). Inducing this extension of

from

to

gives an extension of
\chi
as required in (ii).

After recalling the consequences of
Proposition

5.25
we first check (1), (2) and (4) in each of the four cases for
\phi
. We finally show (3).

Indeed
Proposition

5.25
implies for the character
\phi_{i}
:

\displaystyle T_{0}\left\langle n,\widehat{t}_{i}\right\rangle_{\phi_{i}}=\begin{cases}T_{0}\left\langle n\right\rangle_{\phi_{i}}&\text{if }\phi_{i}\in\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{i},\\
T_{0}\left\langle n\widehat{t}_{i}\right\rangle_{\phi_{i}}&\text{if }\phi_{i}\in\mathbb{D}_{i},\\
T_{0}\left\langle n,\widehat{t}_{i}\right\rangle_{\phi_{i}}&\text{if }\phi_{i}\in{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\sqcup{\mathbb{E}}_{i}^{\prime}.\end{cases}

This leads to

\displaystyle M_{\phi}=\begin{cases}M&\text{if }\phi\in{\mathbb{E}}_{1}^{\prime}.{\mathbb{E}}_{2},\\
M_{0}\left\langle n\right\rangle&\text{if }\phi\in{\mathbb{E}}_{1}.\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{2}\sqcup\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{1}.{\mathbb{E}}_{2},\\
M_{0}&\text{if }\phi_{i}\in\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{1}.\mathbb{D}_{2}.\end{cases}

(5.44)

Hence in all cases
M_{\phi}=M^{\circ}_{\phi}\left\langle n\right\rangle_{\widehat{\phi}}
for
\widehat{\phi}\in\mathrm{Irr}(M^{\circ}_{\phi}\mid\phi)
. According to
Proposition

2.2
(a),
\phi
extends to
M_{\phi}
, whenever
M_{\phi}\neq M
.

Let now first
(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})\in{\mathbb{E}}^{\prime}_{1}\times{\mathbb{E}}_{2}
and hence

. Note that

and

,

and

, see

(d) and (e). Consequently,

and

by the definition of

and

in

.

According to
Proposition

5.25
(b), every extension
\widehat{\phi}_{i}
of
\phi_{i}
to

is
\gamma_{i}
-invariant. This implies
\widehat{\phi}_{i}^{n_{i}^{\circ}}=\widehat{\phi}_{i}
according to

2.23
and then we find a character

that is an extension of
\phi_{i}
. Since

is isomorphic to

(G_{1}\left\langle\widehat{t}_{1},\gamma_{1}\right\rangle/\left\langle h_{0}\right\rangle)\times(G_{2}\left\langle\widehat{t}_{2},\gamma_{2}\right\rangle/\left\langle h_{0}\right\rangle{}),

the character
\psi_{1}.\psi_{2}
first defines a character of
M\left\langle\widehat{t}_{1}\right\rangle
and via restriction we obtain a
\left\langle\widehat{t}_{1}\right\rangle
-invariant character
\chi\in\mathrm{Irr}(M\mid\phi_{1}.\phi_{2})
. We see that
\chi
is an extension of
\phi
to
M
and
\chi^{\widehat{t}_{1}}=\chi
, ensuring (1), (2) and (4).

If
(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})\in\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{1}\times\mathbb{D}_{2}
or equivalently
\phi\in\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{1}.\mathbb{D}_{2}
, then this implies
M_{\phi}=M_{0}
. By Clifford theory (
2.1
), the character

. As

is
\widehat{t}_{2}n
-invariant, the character

seen as a character of

is
\widehat{t}_{1}
-invariant due to

. This ensures (1) and (2).
Considering the action of

on

and using

(see

5.25
(c)), we see that

, ensuring (4).

Assume next that
(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})\in\mathbb{D}_{1}^{\prime}\times{\mathbb{E}}_{2}
and hence
